#2341. Remembering the disaster of 1991/92, Bayern Munich’s worst season in modern history

Mention the words ‘Bayern Munich’ and what comes to mind? A teutonic powerhouse? One of Europe’s elite? Serial winners? The Bavarians are, after all, a team that has won the Bundesliga 31 times – when the first was only 55 years ago.

It is a team that has won the European Cup six times and includes such notable alumni as Franz Beckenbauer, Gerd Muller, Sepp Maier, Karl-Heinz Rummenigge, Lothar Matthaus and Philipp Lahm. It is a world-famous brand that stands for excellence. Bayern Munich equals trophies and success.

But hard as it is to believe, they have endured ups and downs just like all teams – the difference being that a Bayern “down” is still normally what most other teams dream about.

But there is one season that will always live in infamy in Bayern history, a season during the 1990s when they were, frankly speaking, pretty terrible. Of course, terrible for Die Roten is relative – we are not talking about relegation here – but by their lofty standards, they turned into a mediocre team for 12 months. The season in question took place across 1991/92.

What’s more surprising is that there was no immediate sign beforehand of the slump to come. The 1986/87 campaign had seen an Udo Lattek-led Bayern win the Bundesliga, the manager whom Die Roten had dominated Europe under in the 1970s.

That proved to be his final season before being replaced by Jupp Heynckes, who ironically had also replaced Lattek when he became Borussia Monchengladbach manager in 1979. While unable to replicate the Foals’ amazing 1970s success, Heynckes did lead them to fourth-place finishes in 1985 and 1986, followed by third place in 1987 along with a UEFA Cup semi-final, putting him on Bayern’s radar.

Heynckes’ debut season (1987/88) saw Bayern fail to retain their title as an emergent Werder Bremen secured glory under the stewardship of Otto Rehhagel. With strikers Karl-Heinz Riedle and Frank Neubarth, and a defence that conceded just 22 goals in 34 games, Bremen finished four points ahead of second-place Bayern, having beaten them 3-1 in a key week 26 meeting. But normal service was soon resumed as Bayern lifted the Bundesliga the following year while also reaching the semi-finals of the UEFA Cup.

The 1989/90 season saw Bayern even more dominant, again winning the Bundesliga by six points while this time reaching the semi-finals of the European Cup, before falling to the AC Milan of the three Dutch masters. Looking at the squad that season, it is notable that there were no world-renowned stars. The strike duo of Roland Wohlfarth and Alan McInally, while efficient, were hardly names that would’ve had Baresi, Maldini and co worried. But it was still enough to dominate Germany.

As such, the summer of 1990 saw Heynckes strengthen the squad by bringing in Stefan Effenberg, Brian Laudrup and Christian Ziege. Of these, the signing of Effenberg was by far the most interesting. A player who throughout his career would be no stranger to controversy, he was asked about why he had decided to join Bayern. Sporting his blonde mullet, he replied, “Because the other clubs are too stupid to win the Bundesliga.”

It was a surefire way to place a target on your back for the season, a comment sure to have been posted on opposing dressing room walls, including Kaiserslautern’s. An early season league game saw Bayern hammer Kaiserslautern 4-0 at the Olympiastadion before the season then developed into a battle between both teams for the title.

Late March saw the advantage swing to Kaiserslautern as they defeated Bayern 2-1 at home courtesy of a late winner from Stefan Kuntz. That sparked a strong run from Die Roten and, with three matches to go, things were tight.

Bayern then travelled to Bochum to face little-fancied Wattenscheid – a team that played in the tiny Lohrheidestadion and who Bayern had beaten 7-0 earlier in the season. To the shock of a watching nation, Wattenscheid held a 2-1 lead as the game entered the dying minutes. Then, with just three minutes left, the ever-reliable Wohlfarth scored to save Bayern’s blushes. Or so it seemed.

Two minutes later, Thorsten Fink scored a shock winner for the team from Bochum to send 35,000 fans delirious – a defeat that opened the door for Kaiserslautern to claim their first Bundesliga title.

As if the defeat to Wattenscheid wasn’t painful enough, Bayern also suffered heartache in the European Cup. Once again, the semi-final curse raised its head, this time to Eastern European opposition rather than Italian. A strong team had emerged at Red Star Belgrade, who came to Munich and left with a 2-1 win in the first leg of their semi-final clash.

That left Bayern with a tough assignment in front of 80,000 rabid Red Star fans – flares burning in an intimidating atmosphere. But Die Roten rose to the task and the game was about to enter extra-time with Bayern 2-1 up when a freak own goal from captain Klaus Augenthaler in the last minute saw the Yugoslavs grab the all-important equaliser and move on to the final.

So as 1991/92 commenced, Bayern came off disappointment at the end of the preceding season but had still finished second in the league and narrowly missed on a European Cup final appearance. It was a season that many teams would die for.

The squad development continued as Heynckes brought in players such as Jan Wouters, Oliver Kreuzer, Thomas Berthold and two Brazilians, Mazinho and Bernardo. But balancing that was the retirement of long-serving stalwart Augenthaler. Still, Bayern fans were looking forward to reclaiming their position at the top of the league while perhaps claiming a UEFA Cup title.

The opening two league games saw an inauspicious start as Bayern drew at Werder Bremen before losing at home to Bundesliga new boys Hansa Rostock. They then travelled to Dusseldorf and came away with their first win, albeit only 1-0 thanks to a last-minute winner from Mazinho. Fans could start to relax ahead of a straightforward cup tie at home against minnows FC Homburg.

Homburg is a small town in the Saarland region of Germany and their local team had been relatively obscure for most of its history. But, in 1986, they had reached the Promised Land of the Bundesliga for the first time. Their fans enjoyed two seasons at the top table before being relegated and then returning immediately. And so, travelling to Munich, the team was a second-tier outfit who typically played in front of 8,000 fans, even when in the Bundesliga.

Only 9,000 bothered to trek to the Olympiastadion to watch the tie, resulting in a strange atmosphere within the vast bowl. It took 27 minutes but Bayern grabbed the lead through a backpost header from Mazinho. So far, so good. But Homburg emerged in the second half revitalised and had the cheek to equalise via a powerful shot from Rodolfo Cardoso.

Worse was to come for Bayern as, with just 13 minutes remaining, a loose ball fell to Homburg’s Matthias Baranowski, who gladly accepted the gift. This was not in the Bayern script and could not be permitted. Unsurprisingly, while Homburg fans were still rubbing their eyes, Mazinho scored again. Enough of this nonsense.

And so the game went into extra-time when Bayern’s fitness would surely overwhelm the underdogs. But just five minutes in, Homburg’s Michael Kimmel jinked into the area and scored with a low shot. That pesky Homburg side just didn’t know when to lie down. And if that wasn’t bad enough, they then scored again four minutes later through Bernd Gries. Bayern were 4-2 down at home. That was how the game would end. A shocked Bayern had been eliminated in their first cup game by a lower division team in their own stadium.

Sometimes a cup defeat can be a blessing in disguise – at least now they could focus on the league and UEFA Cup. Maybe all was not lost yet.

Their league form continued to fluctuate and, after nine matches, Bayern sat in seventh place as they prepared to commence their continental campaign. A relatively easy first-round draw saw them due to travel to Ireland for a first leg against Cork City. As Bayern fans researched where Cork was on the map, the players flew over.

Cork City had only formed in 1984, following the bankruptcy of Cork United in 1982. A second-place league finish in the previous season had seen the part-timers earn their spot in the UEFA Cup. In front of just 4,000 fans, it was time for Bayern to forget about the Homburg embarrassment and start a successful UEFA Cup campaign.

Dave Barry was a local who enjoyed a successful career with Cork County, playing Gaelic Football and winning two All-Ireland medals. But he also enjoyed regular football on the side and so turned out for Cork City as a midfielder. Ahead of the game, he had somehow come to the attention of Effenberg who, with his normal diplomacy, compared him to his grandfather.

Picked for the game against Bayern, he wrote his name into Irish folklore when, after just 26 minutes, Cork stole the ball from the advancing Bayern defence and Barry found it at his feet. Charging forward, the balding midfielder turned inside one defender before rifling a shot home. The fans went ballistic.

And so it stood until just before half-time when Effenberg finally brought parity to the score. Everyone then braced for the second-half whirlwind from Bayern, but it never occurred. The part-timers of Cork ended with a famous 1-1 draw against the German giants.

The second leg saw Bayern struggle once more. In fact, the Irish part-timers held out until the 71st minute before Bruno Labbadia finally spared Bayern’s blushes. A last-gasp penalty added a little more gloss, but at the final whistle, Die Roten had only just got over the line. Considering this and the Homburg result, all was most definitely not well in Bavaria.

Three days later, Bayern travelled to Stuttgart and were hammered 4-1 – not by VfB but rather by Stuttgarter Kickers. It was finally too much to bear and, three days later, Bayern announced that they were parting company with Heynckes.

The former striking great went on to enjoy a long and successful managerial career, overseeing teams in Spain, Portugal and Germany, including Real Madrid, Benfica, Schalke and his first love, Borussia Monchengladbach. He even returned to Bayern, becoming caretaker manager in 2009 after the firing of Jurgen Klinsmann before replacing Louis van Gaal in 2011 and leading them to a Champions League final defeat to Chelsea.

He then giuded Bayern to glory in 2013 by defeating Bundesliga rivals Borussia Dortmund in the final, making Heynckes just the fourth manager to win the competition with two different teams, Real Madrid being the other. One of the all-time greats, his dismissal after the Kickers defeat was later described by general manager Uli Hoeness as “the biggest mistake I ever made in my business life”.

A meeting was set up with club legend Franz Beckenbauer in Munich’s Sheraton Hotel to offer him the position as head coach. But he declined, stating prior commitments made it impossible. And therefore Bayern turned elsewhere – with a somewhat surprising result.

Replacing Heynckes at the helm would be Danish legend Soren Lerby. A player who enjoyed three seasons with Bayern, during which they won two Bundesliga titles, he had only recently retired from playing at the age of 32. Taking the Bayern job represented his first step into management – a daunting task for experienced heads let alone a rookie. He didn’t even have the required coaching licence at the time of his appointment. It seemed a strange choice to most fans.

His tenure started off shakily as Bayern lost successive league games against Dortmund and VfB Stuttgart to drop to 14th in the 20-team league, just three places above the relegation spots. Next up for Lerby was, ironically, a trip back to Denmark as Bayern had drawn Boldklubben 1903 in the second round of the UEFA Cup. If Lerby thought it would be an enjoyable trip home, he was in for a major surprise.

Not even the most hardcore Boldklubben fans can claim that their team had a grand history. They had won the league seven times, but four of those were before World War Two, while the most recent was back in 1976, before then merging with Kjobenhavns Boldklub in 1992 to form FC Copenhagen, who have enjoyed considerable success since.

They had only ever won two European ties and never made it past the second round. The visit of Bayern would represent one of the last games that Boldklubben would play before the merger. And what a game it was.

Over 14,000 fans packed into the Gentofte Stadion to see how their boys would fare against the German powerhouse. For 30 minutes the game was a stalemate, until Bayern snatched the lead through Mazinho. That lead lasted just five minutes before Michael Manniche tied the game. Tied going into the half-time break, there was little indication of the shockwave to come.

After 57 minutes, Boldklubben earned a penalty, which was duly dispatched by Danish star Ivan Nielsen. Four minutes later, the fans were in ecstasy as Kenneth Wegner made it 3-1. As the ground began to shake, the madness continued; just three minutes later, Manniche grabbed a second to put the Danes 4-1 up. The mighty Bayern had just conceded three goals in the space of seven minutes. And it wasn’t over yet.

Brian Klaus added a fifth before Peter Uldbjerg made it 6-1. No-one could quite believe what they were witnessing. Markus Munch pulled back a last-minute consolation for Bayern but, as the final whistle sounded, all eyes turned to the scoreboard: Boldklubben 1903 6-2 Bayern Munich. Bayern’s season had just truly imploded.

The return leg two weeks later saw Die Roten exit the competition with a whimper, winning just 1-0 through a late Mazinho goal. They had now been eliminated from the DFB-Pokal at home by a lower division side, from the UEFA Cup by Danish minnows and were sitting in 14th place in the league.

Given this start, it was no real surprise that Lerby was a dead man walking. He managed to stumble on until March when a 4-0 defeat away at Kaiserslautern saw Bayern, still stuck in the lower half of the table, finally wield the axe. It was to represent Lerby’s only stint in football management.

It was time for a third manager that season as Hoeness turned to a much more experienced hire in Erich Ribbeck.

Ribbeck had started his managerial career back in 1967 at Rot-Weiss Essen before managing Eintracht Frankfurt, Kaiserslautern, Borussia Dortmund and Bayer Leverkusen. His stats to date were at best mediocre – the only trophy he won during that period was the UEFA Cup with Bayer Leverkusen in 1988 – but he had Bundesliga experience and so was seen as the man who could right the listing ship.

But Bayern by now were in a complete funk and so Ribbeck oversaw a final 11 league games in which they won five but lost as many as they stumbled over the finishing line, mired in mediocrity.

With the season over, Bayern sat in tenth, exactly mid-table. They had lost more games than they had won, including seven at home, and finished with a -2 goal difference. As usual, Wohlfarth was top scorer with 17, followed by Mazinho (12) and Effenberg (11). They had to sit back and watch while VfB Stuttgart, Dortmund and Frankfurt played out final-day drama to captivate the whole country.

All three teams sat on 50 points going into the final matchday and all three were playing away from home. Frankfurt had goal difference advantage and just needed to win, but shockingly lost to Hansa Rostock while having a clear penalty denied to them at 1-1.

Dortmund won at Duisburg and were two minutes from being Bundesliga champions before Guido Buchwald wrote himself into Stuttgart legend with an 88th-minute winner at Leverkusen – despite being down to ten men following the sending-off of Matthias Sammer – earning Stuttgart the title on goal difference.

That summer saw Bayern spend big. In came Thomas Helmer, Jorginho, Mehmet Scholl and, most newsworthy, the return of the prodigal son, Lothar Matthaus. Laudrup and Effenberg were moved on to Fiorentina to help fund the rebuild.

It worked: Bayern finished second in the Bundesliga, just a point behind champions Werder Bremen and with just five losses and a +29 goal difference. Ribbeck had successfully steadied the ship and would soon retire, replaced by Beckenbauer before a disastrous spell as Germany manager from 1998 to 2000. But Ribbeck also left with the unenviable stat of becoming the first and only manager of Bayern to be in charge for over 500 days without winning a single trophy.

To this day, the 1991/92 season stands as a nadir for Bayern. Since the, Die Roten have only ever finished outside the top three twice, and have won 12 Bundesliga titles and three Champions Leagues. While 90s was often an inconsistent decade for Bayern – with players more often featured in the gossip pages rather than the sports pages, leading to the famous “FC Hollywood” nickname – nothing was as bad as 1991/92.

Bayern will always represent one of the elite European teams and are the gold standard against which many others measure themselves. Moreover, they have enjoyed success without the huge external financial backing that some other European sides have enjoyed.

It has been argued by many that their recent dominance of German football has spoilt the Bundesliga as a spectacle, especially considering their ten successive. But it is refreshing to know that even teams like Bayern Munich can experience a season of woe. For that one year, they were actually human after all. And for that, VfB Stuttgart, Homborg, Cork City and Boldklubben will be eternally grateful.

#2339. Loïs Diony marque dans le derby

L'ancien Vert (2017-2020), Loïs Diony, a encore trouvé le chemin des filets à l'occasion de la 10ème journée du championnatde la Cyprus League. Hier, il a ouvert la marque, dès la 5ème minute, lors du derby de Nicosie opposant son équipe, l'Omonia à l'APOEL. Ce derby s'est terminé par un nul et fait reculer les Nicosiotes de l'Omonia d'une place. Ils sont désormais à la 3ème place ex-aequo avec Paphos et l'Apollon Lomssolde rester en haut du classement, ex-aequo à la 3ème place.

En 10 matchs de championnat avec l'Omonia Nicosie, Loïs Diony a déjà inscrit 6 buts.  En 4 matchs de qualification à l'Europa League, il en a inscrit 4. Et en 4 matchs d'Europa League, il en a inscrit 1, il y a quelques jours contre Nimègue. Si l'on fait le bilan de son début de saison à Chypre, Loïs Diony compte donc 11 réalisations en 15 matchs.

#2338. Rayan Boukadida forte tête

Pour son 4ème match en ligue 1 tunisienne, l'ancien Vert, Rayan Boukadida (19 ans) a inscrit son 1er but permettant à son club, l'US Monastir de l'emporter (2-0) sur l'Etoile Sportive du Sahel. Ce but, il l'a inscrit, comme souvent avec les équipes de jeunes de l'ASSE, de la tête suite à un coup-franc venu de la gauche. Il permet à l'US Monastir de grimper à la 4ème place, à 2 points du leader.

Rayan Boukadida, un enfant de Sainté, né à Saint-Etienne en 2007 a rejoint l'ASSE en 2013. Il avait 6 ans. Il a fait toute l'Ecole de Foot, toute la préformation chez les Verts. Il est entré au centre de formation en 2022, et n'a pas été conservé en 2025. Il a rejoint les rangs du Mans FC pour la saison 2025-2026, sa seule saison dans la cité mancelle car, le 2 août 2026, l'US Monastir, club de Ligue 1 tunisienne, a annoncé l'arrivée de l'ancien défenseur central vert. Rayan Boukadida rejoint donc un pays qu'il connaît bien puisqu'il compte 16 sélections dans les équipes U17 à U23 de Tunisie, en attendant mieux. Une belle opportunité pour lui certainement.

Le passage de Rayan Boukadida à l'ASSE n'est pas passé inaperçu car ce puissant défenseur central a la particularité d'avoir inscrit 18 buts en 4 saisons (de U15 à U19): - 2021-2022 : 4 buts avec les U15 de Jean-Philippe Primard- 2022-2023 : 3 buts avec les U17 Nationaux de Patrick Moreau- 2023-2024 : 5 buts avec les U17 de Frédéric Dugand- 2024-2025 : 6 buts avec les U19 de Frédéric Dugand.

#2337. Y'en à Marne ! (5)

Comme le rapporte la dernière édition du Progrès, la préfecture de la Loire a volé hier à la rescousse de celle de la Marne, qui a obligé la veille les supporters stéphanois à rebrousser chemin à quelques encablures du stade Auguste-Delaune, les ayant donc privé d'assister à la victoire des Verts à Reims. Extraits.

"Ce dimanche, la préfecture de la Loire est entrée dans l'arène pour "rapporter plusieurs précisions". L'autorité représentant l'Etat rappelle que "le Conseil d'Etat a rejeté la demande de suspension des arrêtés du Ministre de l'Intérieur et de la préfecture de la Marne. Dès lors les mesures d'interdiction visant les convois collectifs de supporters demeuraient pleinement exécutoires." Elle conteste l'interprétation qui a été faite de la précision du Conseil d'Etat qui aurait fait "de l'accès individuel au stade sans signe distinctif" une autorisation de "déplacement collectif". Selon la jurisprudence, le simple "fait de se rendre en autocar suffit à caractériser la qualité de supporter." Et donc légitime l'intervention des forces de l'ordre qui "n'ont fait qu'appliquer le droit en vigueur et les arrêtés d'interdiction" en renvoyant les supporters chez eux."

Après un début de saison en demi-teinte, il s'installe peu à peu dans son équipe. #2327. Big wins for PSV and Twente close gap on leaders Feyenoord

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

A late equaliser for Fortuna Sittard in De Kuip proved costly for Feyenoord as PSV and Twente racked up huge victories to move within two points of the Eredivisie leaders.

Feyenoord paid the price for failing to finish off the Limburgers, with Luciano Valente’s 59th-minute goal the only reward for their dominance.

Fortuna equalised with four minutes left when Shiloh ’t Zand, on loan from Feyenoord, was allowed to run through to the penalty area and beat Tjark Ernst with a low shot.

Feyenoord coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst made no effort to hide his disappointment. “We know that Fortuna are the kind of team who can score from the few attacks they have,” he said. “If you don’t score the goals, things like this can happen.”

Second-placed Ajax are a point behind after a narrow win at home against AZ ended an 11-match winless streak against the Alkmaar club, as Tolu Arokodare’s early header proved decisive.

Twente recorded the biggest win of the weekend with a 9-0 demolition of Excelsior in Rotterdam, with Wout Weghorst scoring four times and Sondre Ørjasaeter notching up five assists.

Excelsior collapsed following the fifth-minute dismissal of Nesto Groen for a reckless high challenge. The home side were 5-0 down at half-time, with Weghorst claiming a hat-trick in the opening 33 minutes.

PSV bagged seven goals in a one-sided encounter with bottom club Willem II, with all the goals coming from different scorers including Ricardo Pepi, who came on in the second half after a six-week absence through injury.

Telstar opened up a three-point gap at the bottom over ADO Den Haag with a 4-2 win against Utrecht in a painful return to Velsen-Zuid for Telstar’s former coach Anthony Correia.

ADO went down 1-0 in Zwolle, with PEC’s Dylan Mbayo scoring the only goal with a first-time finish on the stroke of half-time.

The match between Cambuur Leeuwarden and Go Ahead Eagles was suspended in the 82nd minute after visiting fans threw fireworks onto the pitch.

The clubs agreed with the KNVB not to play the final minutes and allow Cambuur’s 4-0 advantage to stand as the final result.

Go Ahead’s director Jan-Willem van Dop said the club would take action against those responsible: “I am ashamed on behalf a small group of supporters who ruined things for the club and a large hare of the fans in the away end,” he said.

#2321. Dual passports issue continues to plague Dutch-based footballers

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Dutch-born footballers who were called up to play for former colonies such as Suriname and Indonesia are still in limbo over their national status, six months after the “passportgate” crisis first flared up.

Suriname’s parliament passed a law in September enabling players who are selected for the national team to gain citizenship automatically.

But the Dutch immigration service IND said the law passed in Suriname made no difference to the rules on dual nationality in the Netherlands.

“The fact that Surinamese nationality has been obtained by legal means is not enough on its own to qualify for one of the exceptional categories under our law,” a spokesman for the Dutch justice ministry told broadcaster NOS.

The law was intended to settle the question of dual nationality for footballers, which arose last March when NAC Breda challenged the eligibility of Go Ahead Eagles defender Dean James.

James took Indonesian citizenship last year after accepting an invitation to play for the national team, but under Dutch dual nationality rules he was required to give up his original passport.

The Dutch football association KNVB launched an investigation into the employment status of players who were called up by countries such as Suriname, Cape Verde and Indonesia. In the meantime it arranged for Dutch-based players to be given temporary work permits so they could keep playing for their clubs.

Losing Dutch nationality also stripped them of their EU citizenship, meaning they would need to obtain visas and meet stricter criteria, such as an annual salary threshold of €600,000, to be eligible to play in the Eredivisie.

Several players have passed up invitations to join the Surinamese squad because of the uncertainty, including Danilho Doekhi of Lazio, Milan van Ewijk of Coventry City and Jayden Oosterwolde of Fenerbahçe.

National team manager Henk Fraser said it was “inexplicable” that players’ livelihoods were being damaged by their desire to play international football.

“Both countries have to get together and clarify the situation,” he told NOS. “This is a straightforward case of people following their hearts and wanting to play for Suriname, but not knowing where they stand.”

Some players said they felt misled by the Surinamese football association, which told them they were playing for the national team on “sports passports” that allowed them to remain Dutch citizens.

Former Feyenoord winger Jean-Paul Boëtius said he only discovered after he was first called up for Suriname last year that he had inadvertently renounced his Dutch citizenship as a result.

Shortly afterwards he received a letter from the city council in Rotterdam telling him to hand in his Dutch passport, which he had recently renewed, because it was no longer valid.

“I now have a sticker in my Surinamese passport that says I have a residency permit allowing me to live in the Netherlands,” he told NOS. “But that’s only for a year. It’s a very difficult situation. I want to become Dutch again. I was born and brought up here.”

#2320. Nos prêtés ne sont pas prêts (5) !

Vous l'attendiez, le voici, le voilà, le désormais fameux tour d'horizon des Verts partis s'exiler pour plus (et mieux) jouer.

Tandis que leurs anciens coéquipiers ont frappé un grand coup en ramenant les 3pts de Reims, les joueurs prêtés par l’ASSE ont joué plus que d’ordinaire ce week-end, à défaut de briller particulièrement. Bonne nouvelle en effet, ils étaient 5 sur 9 alignés d’entrée en attendant le match du Rapid Bucarest ce soir.

Du côté des championnats étrangers, Igor Miladinovic confirme son statut de pilier au Partizan Belgrade. Il a ainsi vécu sa 12e titularisation en championnat ce week-end pour une nouvelle défaite sur la pelouse de l’MT Belgrade (1-2) dans un des nombreux derbies de la capitale serbe. Il totalise désormais 969 minutes de jeu (plus 113 en Coupe d’Europe) et le Partizan Belgrade reste 10e sur 14 après 16 journées.

Son voisin Strahinja Stojkovic a connu une troisième titularisation de suite (la 5e cette saison), pour une troisième défaite (0-2) avec l’OFK Belgrade désormais avant dernier du championnat. Stojkovic est donc relégable et compte désormais 386 minutes de jeu

En Roumanie, avant le match du Rapid Bucarest ce soir, le temps de jeu de Annan restait réduit : 241 minutes en championnat, et une dernière titularisation (la 3e) qui remonte au 18 septembre.

Direction l’Espagne ou Lucas Stassin réalise un début de saison mitigé. Il était certes de nouveau titulaire avec le FC Séville dans le derby face au Betis et s’est montré actif, mais il n’a pas eu l’occasion de briller et est sorti à la vilaine 69e minute, avant que son équipe égalise (1-1). Il compte désormais 541 minutes de jeu en Liga en 8 matchs (6 titularisations et 2 sorties de banc) et a marqué 1 but et délivré 1 passe décisive.

En d2 espagnole, les choses se compliquent pour Aïmen Moueffek. Son équipe réagit … mais sans lui. Pour la 2e fois de suite, il est resté scotché sur le banc, et Cadix l’a emporté 3-0 sur la pelouse de l’équipe B de la Real Sociedad. Son bilan reste de 202 minutes de jeu en championnat (3 titularisations) et 45 en coupe, alors qu’ont déjà été jouées 15 journées de championnat.

Dans l’Hexagone, l’éclaircie bourguignonne se confirme : En effet, si Djylian N’Guessan a de nouveau vu son équipe s’imposer depuis le banc de touche et continue d’afficher un très maigre temps de jeu avec Brest (28 minutes de jeu depuis le début de saison), Pierre Ekwah a connu sa seconde titularisation de suite, avec un nul (1-1) décroché par Auxerre à Monaco.  Il totalise désormais 223 minutes de jeu.

A Pau toujours rien de neuf pour Luan Gadegbeku (photo) qui, après sa titularisation en Coupe, est retourné sur le banc ce week-end. Il reste sur son maigre total de 147 minutes de jeu (plus 90 en Coupe). En revanche Axel Dodote a connu une 7e titularisation sur la pelouse du Red Star avec Pau, la première depuis début septembre, profitant notamment de l’absence de Briançon en défense centrale. Hélas il est sorti à la 53e . Son temps de jeu est désormais de 526 minutes.

#2317. Ces Lorrains ne sont pas des quiches

Après 13 journées, l’ASNL a déjà empoché plus de points (28) que sur l’ensemble du championnat de L2 de la saison 2021-2022 ponctuée par sa relégation en National (27 points) ! Restant sur une impressionnante série de 7 victoires avant de défier les leaders stéphanois le 5 décembre dans le Chaudron pour le match au sommet de la 14e journée, les surprenants dauphins nancéiens viennent d’égaler la plus grande série de succès de l’histoire du club au chardon. Cela faisait 52 ans que Nancy n’avait pas réussi une telle performance. Lors de cet exercice 1974-1975, un certain Michel Platini (19 ans à l’époque) avait réussi sa première saison pleine en claquant 17 pions en 32 matches de D2. Il s’était aussi illustré cette saison-là en marquant pas moins de 13 buts en Coupe de France dont 3 en 8e de finale contre son futur club stéphanois, qui filait alors vers son dernier doublé de l’histoire.

Oswald Tanchot commente la magnifique dynamique de son équipe dans L’Est Républicain. "Notre force, c’est d’avoir un vestiaire aussi sain. On a des joueurs qui sont dans une logique de collectif en permanence, ça se voit sur le terrain et ça nous aide à aller chercher les victoires. Les joueurs qui entrent dans l’équipe, que ce soit au coup d’envoi ou en cours de match, apportent souvent quelque chose. Il faut absolument garder cet état d’esprit. Il y a beaucoup de solidarité dans notre jeu, des fermetures de passes pour l’adversaire parce que tous nos joueurs font les efforts dans le replacement. Ces petits détails font une grande différence. On voulait se donner le droit d’avoir une affiche magnifique à Geoffroy-Guichard. Mais avant de penser à Saint-Etienne, on souhaite négocier au mieux ce 8e tour de Coupe de France contre Furiani (National 1)."

#2315. Contribute to help victims of the Pedrógão Grande tragedy

Forest fires occur every summer in Portugal but the tragedy that struck the community of Pedrógão Grande in the centre of the country is unprecedented. Over 60 people lost their lives, many trapped in their cars when trying to flee from the flames.

This Just Giving page is a simple way for people, wherever they are in the world, to make a contribution to help rebuild these communities and bring some comfort to those who lost everything in the blink of an eye. The money raised will be sent to a network of local authorities and charities working in the area affected. Thank you for your support.

#2314. Feyenoord facing fireworks fine after 3-0 loss to Internazionale

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Feyenoord are facing a heavy fine from European football authorities after fireworks were let off during their Champions League defeat by Internazionale.

Referee Glenn Nyberg suspended the match for several minutes after flares were fired onto the roof from outside De Kuip stadium, damaging the structure. Police later made two arrests.

“It’s obviously not right and it’ll be another fine,” coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst said wearily after the game, which ended in a comprehensive 3-0 defeat for his team.

One flare landed on the pitch despite the club taking measures such as hanging nets from the gantries to stop objects being thrown from the stands. “They’re letting off fireworks from outside now,” Van Bronckhorst said. “What do you do about that?”

Uefa are likely to hit Feyenoord with a fine and could order the club to close parts of the stadium for their next home matches in the competition, against Porto on November 24 and RB Leipzig on January 27.

The club also have a suspended penalty hanging over them from last October, when they were fined €60,000 after fans let off fireworks during the match against Greek club Panathinaikos.

On the pitch, Feyenoord were undone by a classic display of Italian defending and counter-attacking, as Internazionale sat back after taking an eighth-minute lead through Yann Bisseck.

Francesco Pio Esposito headed in a second early in the second half, just as Feyenoord were pushing for an equaliser, before Piotr Zielinski made sure of the win with a shot from 20 metres.

Captain Luciano Valente said the result was “more than deserved” and the visitors had shown more “quality”.

“This defeat was harder to take than the one against Barcelona,” he told Ziggo Sport. “Because we thought going in that we could get something, but we just weren’t good enough.”

Van Bronckhorst said he was not surprised by the difference in class between the teams. “They scored the first goal out of nothing,” he said. “And Inter know better what the game needs at every stage.

“Their players have a lot of experience, they’ve been in the final twice in the last four years. So we’re at a disadvantage.”

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV Eindhoven endured a frustrating night in the Champions League as their defensive frailties were exposed in a 2-1 defeat by Club Brugge.

PSV started full of attacking intent and should have taken an early lead when Ivan Perisic put Sergiño Dest clean through on goal, but the defender’s shot was too close to goalkeeper Yann Sommer.

Instead it was the visitors who took the lead with their first real attack when Nicolo Tresoldi headed in a teasing cross from Joaquin Seys.

PSV equalised early in the second half as an outstanding pass by Sven Mijnans into the path Lutsharel Geertruida allowed the defender to charge to the byline. His pullback found its way to Kodai Sano, whose shot took a deflection off Seys into the net.

But five minutes later PSV were caught out as another razor-sharp pass by Seys released Carlos Forbs in space to net the winner.

The home side refused to give up, but were unable to find a way past Sommer in the closing stages as the Belgian side closed out the win.

Team captain Mauro Junior admitted that PSV had been undone by shortcomings at the back. “We had a lot of opportunities in the first half but we didn’t score,” he told Ziggo Sport.

“We want to attack and score goals, but we weren’ well organised today in defence. We need to keep our heads up and look forward to the next game.”

The result leaves PSV in 24th place, just above the elimination line, ahead of their next match away to Real Madrid on November 24, with four points from their opening four matches.

#2292. Top five football betting tips and tricks

Football is the world’s most famous sport, and it is the sport of choice of a great many sports punters as well. Especially popular in Europe, betting on football can be an incredibly profitable enterprise, so long as you know how to do it.

Getting started on football betting is far from easy, though. This form of gambling requires dedication, investment, and persistence. As you gain experience, your intuition will improve and you will be able to make more correct predictions.

But experience is not the only thing that can help you become a better football punter. In fact, you can substantially improve your betting strategy by simply following some basic football betting tips.

You might find shopping around boring and unhelpful, but you just have to do it when you search sites for football betting. As you know, the higher the odds, the bigger the payout. What you may have missed, however, is that odds are different at each bookmaker. Having said that, shopping around for the best odds is sensible, as it rewards you with bigger payouts.

In this line of thought, sticking to one bookmaker is virtually pointless. Even if the bookie of your choosing offers the best odds for a number of matches, it is highly unlikely that their odds will remain the best for long.

An obvious one, we know. And yet, you will be surprised by the number of football bettors who wager on football, but do not follow the sport. So, why is it important to follow the action closely?

First of all, by watching football matches, you can take part in the most exciting football betting market – the live betting market. What is more important, however, is that following the action provides an invaluable insight into teams and players’ style, condition, and determination – important factors  to consider when placing a bet.

As you watch more and more games, you will improve your analytic abilities. In time, you will be able to make more accurate predictions about the outcome of different matches, which, of course,  will result in placing more winning bets.

As you know, odds are based on the probability of occurrence of a certain outcome; the higher the odds, the more unlikely it is for that outcome to actually occur. But what happens when the odds offered by the bookmaker represent a probability that is lower than the actual probability of an outcome occurring? In such a situation, you can place what is called a value bet.

If you want to become a professional football punter, you should always search for value when betting, as it leads to huge profits

Favoured by beginners in particular, accumulators are a type of football wager which allows you to bet on as many fixtures as you like. Most commonly, you will be asked to choose a match winner, but as the market is diverse, you may be offered a number of other betting options. What makes accumulators an appealing betting market is the huge potential payout – here, you can stake as little as £1 and have the potential to win more than £500,000, depending on the bookmaker and the number of selected fixtures.

If you find accumulators’ payouts too good to be true, you are right. As a matter of fact, there is a reason for the odds to be set so high – actually winning an accumulator bet, especially one which involves a wide selection of matches, is nearly impossible. You see, all you need for your whole wager to lose is one wrong prediction. For example, if you bet on 10 matches and just one of them goes wrong, your entire accumulator is lost.

To make money by betting on football, avoid betting on accumulators – the risk is simply not worth it.

Finally, if you want to be a successful football punter, learn how not to mix emotions with betting. This is easier said than done, though.

Whenever your favourite team plays, you want them to win, regardless of how (im)possible that may be. If you do not learn how to leave emotions out of betting, however, you are highly likely to put money on your favourite team even if they have no chance of winning the match. Conversely, should you base your bets on hard facts and not on emotions, you increase your chances to win.

Our advice – avoid betting on fixtures that involve one of your favourite teams, that is the easiest way to keep emotions at bay.

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV returned to the top of the Eredivisie with a thumping 5-2 win over Cambuur Leeuwarden as previous leaders Feyenoord lost a seven-goal thriller against the other Frisian club, Heerenveen.

It took just 46 seconds for PSV to get off the mark as Sven Mijnans headed in unmarked.

The midfielder added a second after 15 minutes and completed his hat-trick with a free-kick in the 80th minute to make the score 4-2, just as Cambuur were threatening a comeback.

Feyenoord’s dip in form continued as they let a two-goal lead slip at Heerenveen. The Rotterdammers looked to be in control going into the break with a trademark drilled shot by Anis Hadj Moussa and a penalty by Nacho Ferri.

But Maas Willemsen bundled in the ball from a corner on the stroke of half-time, before Ringo Meerveld came on for the second half and scored a hat-trick as Feyenoord’s defence crumbled under pressure.

“In the matches when we played well I saw a team that fought for each other,” Feyenoord coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst said after the game. “Mentally strong, kept running even when we didn’t have the ball. That’s our strength. When we don’t do that we’re a lesser team.”

Mexx Meerdink scored a hotly contested late penalty to settle a tempestuous game in Alkmaar as AZ came back from 3-1 down against Groningen to claim their first win in five matches.

Goalkeeper Etienne Vaessen was given a second yellow card for protesting too hard against the decision to penalise him for upending Ayoub Oufkir. Once the commotion died down Meerdink, who had missed from the spot against Vaessen earlier in the second half, made no mistake against substitute goalkeeper Lovro Stubljar.

Ajax missed the chance to take over top spot at Twente as their former striker Wout Weghorst scored a last-minute penalty to rescue a point for the home side.

Ajax had been 2-0 up with a bullet header by their giant striker Tolu Arokodare and a long-range strike by Rayane Bounida, before an own goal by Dies Janse brought the visitors back into the game.

Fortuna Sittard also had to settle for a point against Telstar after going 2-0 up thanks to a bizarre own goal by Sem Valk. The defender’s sliced clearance looped over the head of Ronald Koeman Junior as the goalkeeper tried to head it away rather than risk being penalised for handling a backpass.

Goals just before half-time by Abdelnour Soualhia and Patrick Brouwer brought Telstar back into the game, but neither side was able to find a winner after the break.

That result allowed ADO, under the guidance of interim coach Levi Schwiebbe, to close the gap on 16th-placed Telstar to one point with a 3-1 win against Sparta, while bottom Willem II collected their third point of the season at home to PEC Zwolle.

#2280. Contre les Caladois, jamais deux sans trois ?

#2279. Opinion: The morality of football

Yesterday evening, Selecao correspondent Nathan Motz accepted a unique opportunity to appear as a guest on the Moral Maze – a BBC radio broadcast dealing with matters of moral and ethical significance.

Is football a moral force for good? That was the question of the day, and each of the four guests were asked to discuss various aspects of the debate from financial fairplay to club takeovers by foreign entities to racism.

Nathan’s segment was concerned with whether football has transcendent moral value, and what that is exactly. Is there a greater significance to the game we are watching, or is it merely 22 humans kicking a leather ball into a net?

Join Nathan as he elaborates on the most significant takeaways of what was a fascinating discussion on the sport and morality in general.

Football, for some, may be the most poignant reminder of the moral universe in which we live. While debate has raged for centuries regarding the precise origin of moral absolutes and how humans ought to live, it is simply unavoidable to say humanity is often troubled by events surrounding the game we all love so dearly.

In not so distant history, every normal act of human life would have been assigned some degree of moral significance. Every action had inherently moral indicators - for good or for evil. Today, postmodern views of morality dominate. These describe morality as an entirely subjective dimension. In other words, it cannot be pinned down exactly whether behaviors are objectively good or bad. We can only find fault with someone else’s interpretation based on their own unique moral code. Morality is relative to the individual or society.

Yet last night’s dialogue concerning football and human behavior was filled with moral accusations and claims which strongly suggested humans are not content with relativist morality. Indeed, we are at times frightened by and disgusted with what we observe in and around football. Our sense of moral justice is deeply disturbed. But with respect to what exactly?

In responding to the panelist’s questions, I observed a familiar paradigm. Each time I have discussed any aspect of moral behavior, the speculation humans are good or at worst morally neutral animals continually reemerges. This widely accepted presupposition is then followed shortly by disgust at some aspect of human behavior. Almost as if to ask “why are all these good humans behaving so badly?” while ignoring the very real implications of this line of inquiry. A question rendered unanswerable by the insistent a priori assumptions from which it was derived.

If humans are intrinsically beneficent, conscientious, fair, humble, and honest then why is football rife with racism, misogyny, greed, disenfranchisement, lies, cheating, violence, and the hypocrisy to deny the occurrence of any such behaviors except when a rival club is the guilty party? Behaviors which are perpetrated by individual supporters, fanatical groups, club administrations, corporate bodies such as FIFA, political entities, nations, and even the players themselves. No one participating faction is guiltless.

To so blithely sidestep more sufficient explanations for the empirical reality of wickedness in football and in human behavior more generally is not just factually incorrect. It undermines real attempts to understand the root of these behaviors where they might be dealt with more effectively.

To listen to The Moral Maze episode entitled “Football: a moral force for good?” produced by BBC radio and featuring PortuGOAL’s Seleção correspondent Nathan Motz click on this link

Furthermore, in good faith, how successful is a response against heinous evil when those perpetrating it are regarded as behaving in accord with their unique moral ambitions? Humans refuse to accept their indisputably objective moral lacking at a fundamental level. This restricts us from ever doing any more than “stamping out” the alleged impropriety of others – while we ourselves may readily engage in these very same behaviors. Moral relativism cannot account for the moral outrage emanating from all corners of the football world. Is Qatar wrong to perpetrate human rights atrocities or is it allowable because their culture is indifferent? Is hooliganism objectively wrong, or only when someone else’s club is responsible?

The overlooking of our own failings while lashing out at others for theirs is quintessential to the morally fallen nature of humanity itself. Football tells all. There are not good people and bad people. There are only people who both willfully and unintentionally transgress the moral code embedded in each of our hearts. A moral law which can only have ever been given by a moral Lawgiver. Otherwise, it is truly your word against mine as it pertains to the moral validity of all behaviors - even those which may deeply violate your conscience.

Football generates an episodic, semantic, and imagistic description of universal archetypes: good and evil, sacrifice and greed, cruelty and mercy, hero and anti-hero. It speaks to every arc of life’s plotline: tragedy, comedy, victory, defeat, the finite nature of our circumstances. It is one of the preeminent existential dialects alongside religion, visual art, written and spoken language, music, and theatre. Football plucks a primal chord.

As metaphysics have been curtailed to just physics in the modern secular West, football has only elevated our awareness of the often dire moral predicaments of our species. Yet it is clear the moral conversation does not originate on a football pitch. The pitch is but a grand stage upon which moral themes may be observed and critiqued, a mirror held in front of our own soul.

The desire for ultimate purpose, for meaning beyond the material realm, never disappeared in modernity. Arguably, it has only become more desperate as the outcry for ultimate significance comes under more scrutiny and criticism by those who constrain ontological explanations to those accounted for by physics. The severity of the mental health crisis in the West is in no small part explained by the terrifying existential awareness we are cut adrift in an abstract sea of meaningless, hopeless wandering between two oblivions - that before mortal life began and that at mortal life’s end. Let football be one voice which reassures you - there is ultimate meaning and good reasons why football so unsettles our craving for justice.

In a palpable, unavoidable way, football and supporter communities across Europe are encountering moral quandaries. In Portugal, age-old debates between FC Porto and Benfica have routinely been haunted by, in many cases, farcical and cynical hypocrisy. Throughout its history, the FPF has been susceptible to showing partiality to a club, player, or style which ultimately degrades the performance of our beloved Seleção. Deny it all we like, and not one aspect of this reality is changed.

In conclusion, whatever your thoughts or beliefs on the subject of moral significance, its origin, the meaning of life, and how football draws that out, I hope it will be beneficial to examine these matters more deeply. In my zeal, I admit I have not always been prudent to initiate these discussions in a way which respects thoughtful disagreement. It is my hope this effort to bring that discussion to Portuguese football will be well-received and carefully pondered. But I will contentedly listen to those followers who may be genuinely unhappy or unsupportive of this or any other idea about which I write.

The readers of this website and the larger Portuguese football loving world are my people. It is a joy and a responsibility I take seriously whenever I have the opportunity to raise important questions about the Selecao, the game, and our lives in general.

#2276. Johan Cruyff’s grandson called up to play for USA under-18 team

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

The most famous name in Dutch football could appear on an international men’s team sheet for the first time in 30 years after Johan Cruyff’s grandson was called up for the United States’ upcoming matches in Europe.

Shae Cruijff-Beane, the son of Johan Cruyff’s daughter Chantal and her American husband Todd Beane, was included in the squad that will play against Norway and Portugal in the next seven days.

Cruijff-Beane plays as a midfielder for Gimnàstic de Tarragona, a club in the Spanish third tier based near Barcelona. His older brother Jordan also came through the club’s ranks and is currently playing US college football at Westmont College in California.

Shae, 17, is eligible to play for the USA through his father and Spain and the Netherlands through his mother, who spent part of her childhood in Catalonia while Johan Cruyff was playing for Barcelona in the 1970s.

The USA under-18 squad also includes an Ajax player, midfielder Xander Newstead, who grew up in San Francisco and joined the Amsterdam club’s youth team set-up at the age of 10, beforec signing professional terms last year.

Johan Cruyff went on to play in the United States for Los Angeles Aztecs and Washington Diplomats before returning to the Netherlands, winding down his career at Ajax and Feyenoord.

His son Jordi played for Barcelona and Manchester United and won nine international caps in 1996. Last year he was appointed technical director at Ajax, the club where his father began his career.

#2275. Villefranche, la pire défense de France

Après avoir affronté et péniblement éliminé la meilleure attaque de National 1, Sainté va défier samedi (19h) dans le Chaudron la pire défense de Ligue 3.

En effet, si l’ASSE a sur le papier le tirage le moins favorable des clubs de Ligue 2 (les 11 autres rescapés affrontent des clubs de N1, N2 ou Regionale), on notera que Villefranche est bien mal en point dans sa division. Au point que le club situé 30km au nord de Lyon a changé de coach début octobre, en enrôlant Hakim Malek (ex-coach de Martigues alors en L2).

Actuellement 17e sur 18 avec 2 victoires, 2 nuls et déjà 9 défaites, Villefranche s’est incliné (2-4) ce week-end à Bastia, alors que le club semblait aller un peu mieux depuis l’arrivée de Malek qui, avant cette défaite avait pris 4pts en 3 matchs de championnat et battu aisément Paray le monial (formation de R2) en Coupe (3-0).

Néanmoins, avec 30 buts encaissés en 13 matchs (soit 8 de plus que la 2e pire défense de la L3 !), Villefranche bat des records, bien au-delà de la pire défense de L2 (Rodez, 24 buts encaissés) et de L1 (Auxerre et Le Havre, 25 buts encaissés), mais également plus que toute autre équipe de N1 ou N2 (qui ont certes disputé moins de journées). Outre la défaite à Bastia, deux autres fessées subies à l’extérieur en septembre à Fleury (1-6) et au Puy (1-4) confirment en particulier la fragilité des Caladois loin de chez eux.

Cette très poreuse défense ne nous est pas totalement inconnue puisqu’y évoluent deux anciens verts (Marvin Tshibuabua très récemment rejoint par Maedine Makhloufi prêté par Dunkerque) qu'on imagine très motivés à l'idée de faire mentir ces chiffres.

#2269. Dutch progress in Nations League despite 5-0 drubbing in Germany

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

The Netherlands came crashing down to earth after hammering Greece as they were outclassed and outmuscled by Germany in Berlin.

Oranje went into the final match of the Nations League group stage with high hopes of recording their first win over their old rivals in seven matches stretching back to 2019.

Instead they suffered their second worst defeat ever at the hands of Germany, but still progressed to the knockout stages thanks to their thumping 7-0 win in Amsterdam on Friday.

Coach Xavi Hernandez was forced to make one change in midfield as Jurriën Timber replaced Barcelona’s Frenkie de Jong, who pulled out with an obturator muscle strain.

Germany shaded the first half and went ahead five minutes before the break when Freiburg striker Derry Scherhant cut in from the left hand side and beat Bart Verbruggen with a curling strike into the top corner.

Scherhant had struck the post minutes earlier, while the Dutch might have taken the lead earlier when Crysencio Summerville slipped in the penalty area as he tried to control a cross by Cody Gakpo.

But in the second half the Dutch fell apart. In the 60th minute Florian Wirtz shoved Denzel Dumfries off the ball and had space to tee up a left-footed shot from the edge of the area that gave Verbruggen no chance.

Nico Schlotterbeck added a third from a free-kick five minutes later, before Wirtz drifted past Gakpo on the right side of the penalty area to score his second.

The humiliation was complete when Brajan Gruda capped a fine attacking performance by firing in a rebound.

Dumfries made no excuses afterwards for what he admitted was a “terrible performance”. “We gave the ball away too easily, left too much space and were too weak in the challenges,” he told NOS. “We can’t let that happen.”

Netherlands U21’s have one foot in the European Championships after a 3-0 win in Denmark in the first leg of their playoff.

After finishing second in their group, the Netherlands need to overcome Denmark in a two-legged playoff to seal their spot in the Euros.

Michael Reiziger handed debuts to Aaron Bouwman and Don-Angelo Konadu, while Kees Smit was not fit enough to take part.

The Netherlands were in excellent form on Friday as they hammered Greece 7-0 to all but seal their spot in the Nations League Quarter-finals.

Xavi had Frenkie de Jong back in the midfield as he partnered Joey Veerman in midfield. Jurrien Timber played left-back as Micky van de Ven replaced the missing Virgil van Dijk. Up front, Donyell Malen led the line.

The first half was a joy to behold for Oranje fans as Netherlands ran rampant with five goals. Crysencio Summerville got the party started with a fine finish in the sixth minute. Veerman then sent an excellent through ball for Tijjani Reijnders to make it 2-0.

The Netherlands were playing lovely football with Veerman, Timber and Malen all involved. It was quickly 3-0 as Reijnders tapped in before the midfielder completed his hattrick in the 33rd minute. Before the break, Timber added the fifth.

In the second half, Teun Koopmeiners replaced De Jong but the Netherlands kept pushing forward and Timber got his second to make it 6-0. Malen then got amongst the goals for the seventh on the hour mark.

Xavi then rang more changes with Ruben van Bommel, Noa Lang, Gjivai Zechiel and Ian Maatsen all coming on. The changes took away some of the momentum and there was no further goals before the end.

The Netherlands are now almost guaranteed a place in the quarter-finals and only a huge loss to Germany on Monday would prevent this.

#2260. Ajax go second in Eredivisie behind PSV as Feyenoord woes deepen

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Ajax moved into a clear second place in the Eredivisie with a routine 3-0 win against ADO Den Haag, as Feyenood slipped to fifth after losing to local rivals Excelsior.

Davy Klaassen took just three minutes to stroke home the opening goal for Ajax and added a second just before half-time.

By then ADO had been reduced to 10 men following the dismissal of Matteo Waem for hauling down Tolu Arakodare.

But there was a blow for Ajax ahead of next weekend’s visit to Feyenoord when Jorthy Mokio, scorer of the second goal, was taken off injured after an accidental collision with Sékou Sylla.

Feyenoord, who started the month top of the Eredivisie, suffered their first home defeat in the derby against Excelsior since 1980 to continue a slump in form that has seen them draw two and lose four of their last six games.

Givairo Read’s blistering shot gave Feyenoord the lead after just two minutes, but Excelsior were ahead by half-time and made it 3-1 when Casper Windell headed in a corner after 58 minutes. Despite an own goal by Jan Plug, on loan from Feyenoord, Excelsior were not to be denied.

PSV remain a point clear of Ajax at the top of the table after they cruised to a 4-1 win at NEC, with Filip Kostic opening the scoring with his first goal for PSV and his first Eredivisie goal since 2014, when he was playing for Groningen.

Utrecht sacked their managed Anthony Correia after just 14 matches after sinking to 15th place in the table with a 3-2 home defeat to Cambuur.

Correia won just four games after leaving Telstar, the club he led to promotion two seasons ago and where he spent his entire 15-year playing career. Youth team coach Mark Otten will take charge of the first team while Utrecht search for a replacement.

Willem II, searching for their first Eredivisie win in 30 matches, took a shock lead away to Twente despite being reduced to 10 men in the first half when Devin Haen was given space in the area to shoot past Joël Drommel.

The home side struggled to turn their superiority into goals, but an equaliser by Ramiz Zerrouki and a late header by Marko Pjaca gave them the points they needed to rise to third place above Feyenoord.

AZ also overtook Feyenoord as they beat Sparta 5-1 with a dominant second-half display, while Fortuna Sittard kept in touch with the leading group with a typically gritty 1-0 win at Go Ahead Eagles.

#2258. L'ASSE dispose d'atouts considérables

Directeur général adjoint de l'ASSE en charge de la marque, du marketing et de la communication, Cédric Chambaz s'exprime dans le hors série de Décideurs Corporate Finances. Extraits.

"Il y a un changement macroéconomique dans l’industrie du football. L’ère du chef d’entreprise qui veut s’offrir une danseuse en investissant ses fonds propres pour se faire plaisir le week-end est derrière nous. Aujourd’hui, nous sommes dans une logique d’investissement beaucoup plus raisonnée, même si elle laisse toute sa place à la passion. Quand Larry Tannenbaum mandate Ivan Gazidis pour trouver un club de football dans lequel investir, il recherche un actif à fort potentiel sur un marché mûr. Il ne s’agit donc pas du tout de mécénat à fonds perdus. Pour autant, cet investissement porte sur un produit d’émotion, et l’AS Saint-Étienne dispose d’atouts considérables en la matière.

Nous sommes un club patrimonial, une institution du football européen. Historiquement, nous sommes une des équipes les plus titrées de France et nous bénéficions d’un engouement qui dépasse largement notre territoire. Nos supporters sont répartis dans toute la France et notre fréquentation au stade est totalement décorrélée de notre niveau sportif actuel. Nous affichons la sixième affluence du football français, alors que nous évoluons sportivement en deuxième division.

Pour Kilmer Sport Ventures, l’ASSE présentait des caractéristiques intéressantes mais le club était aussi une sorte de géant endormi qui, ces dernières années, n’avait pas bénéficié des investissements de modernisation nécessaires pour rester compétitif dans le football contemporain. L’idée est donc de réveiller le club grâce à des investissements ciblés, fondés sur une analyse approfondie de l’existant et de nos objectifs afin de repositionner l’AS Saint-Étienne comme une institution, sur le terrain comme en dehors.

Un club de football peut être considéré comme un média. Plus son exposition est forte, dans les médias traditionnels comme sur les réseaux sociaux, plus il peut valoriser son inventaire commercial, c’est-à-dire les espaces qu’il propose à ses sponsors. À l’ASSE, nous bénéficions d’une audience nationale élevée. C’est ce qui nous rend attractifs pour le diffuseur, même en Ligue 2. Nous travaillons aussi à valoriser cette audience sur les réseaux sociaux, où nous rassemblons plus de 2,9 millions de fans sur l’ensemble de nos plateformes.

Aujourd’hui, nous sommes capables de générer en France l’une des 10 plus fortes valorisations médiatiques pour nos annonceurs, et ce malgré notre présence en deuxième division. C’est notamment le fruit du travail mené en communication et en marketing pour faire rayonner l’équipe au-delà du stade.

L’enjeu, au-delà de la seule billetterie, est de transformer notre notoriété en revenus. Cela passe d’abord par notre ancrage régional. Nous devons continuer à attirer des supporters au stade et à remplir Geoffroy-Guichard. Mais nous devons aussi toucher ceux qui ne peuvent pas venir au stade, en développant notre marque et nos produits dérivés. Cela suppose de travailler notre présence sur les réseaux sociaux, de valoriser les joueurs et de construire leur image, notamment lorsqu’ils sont issus de notre centre de formation. Certains disposent déjà de communautés importantes, capables de contribuer à la vente de maillots et d’ouvrir de nouveaux marchés. À défaut, c’est au club de construire cette visibilité.

Notre ambition est de conquérir de nouveaux publics, notamment en Europe, mais chaque chose en son temps. Notre audience dépasse aujourd’hui notre territoire régional, mais nous ne pouvons pas prétendre rivaliser aujourd’hui avec le PSG à l’échelle internationale. Cela passera d’abord par le retour en Ligue 1 et par des performances sportives à la hauteur de nos ambitions. C’est toute la complexité de notre activité. Nous pouvons bâtir une stratégie marketing solide et développer nos outils de fidélisation, mais les résultats sportifs restent déterminants. Si vous perdez cinq matchs de suite, la dynamique commerciale s’en ressent forcément. Le projet sportif reste le juge de paix, même si nous devons apprendre à développer nos revenus au-delà du seul résultat du week-end.

La grande différence entre être DAF ou CMO d’une start-up dans la tech et dans un club comme Saint-Etienne ? A mon sens, elle tient à l’attachement des supporters envers le club et à la manière dont il influence leur vie. Très peu d’entreprises peuvent se targuer de voir leur logo tatoué sur le corps d’un de leurs clients et, pourtant, c’est le cas de l’ASSE. En outre, lorsqu’on se promène dans la ville, tous les acteurs économiques et politiques ont un avis sur la situation du club. Dans une région industrielle en difficulté, cette proximité et l’intensité de cette relation sont primordiales pour rassembler la population. Cette intensité m’a vraiment surpris, par comparaison avec les autres entreprises où j’ai travaillé. Le club cristallise des espoirs, des projections de succès qui transcendent le football."

#2256. « Je suis candidat à l'élection présidentielle de 2027 » : L'annonce inattendue de Teddy Riner !

C'est une annonce pour le moins inattendue dans le monde du sport tricolore. Ce lundi 9 novembre 2026, Teddy Riner a officialisé sa candidature à l'élection présidentielle de 2027 dans une vidéo publiée sur son compte X, tout en lançant son propre mouvement politique intitulé « La France qu'on veut ».

Le judoka français de 37 ans se lance ainsi en politique après avoir dominé le judo international. Quintuple champion olympique et onze fois champion du monde, Teddy Riner a bâti un palmarès exceptionnel qui le place au sommet de sa discipline. Alors que son avenir sportif restait au cœur des discussions depuis les Jeux de Paris, le Français surprend en ouvrant un tout autre chapitre personnel. Un virage spectaculaire.

Dans une vidéo publiée ce lundi 9 novembre 2026 sur son compte X, Teddy Riner a pris la parole face caméra afin de lever le voile sur ses intentions pour la prochaine présidentielle. Le judoka de 37 ans a officialisé sa démarche avec des mots simples et directs.

Conscient des réactions que son entrée en politique peut susciter, le quintuple champion olympique a tenu à devancer immédiatement les critiques concernant son manque d'expérience dans les fonctions publiques. Teddy Riner a ainsi déclaré : « Je sais ce qu'on va dire de moi. Il est fou, il n'est pas du sérail, il n'a pas les codes, il n'a jamais fait de politique. Mais posez-vous la question avec moi, qu'est-ce qui est fou aujourd'hui ? Je n'ai pas passé ma vie derrière un public, mais le combat a toujours fait partie de moi. » Une détermination affichée.

Pour concrétiser ses ambitions, le judoka français de 37 ans a également fondé sa propre formation politique ce lundi 9 novembre 2026, baptisée « La France qu'on veut ». Cette prise de parole sur les réseaux sociaux a d'ailleurs été accompagnée d'un entretien accordé au quotidien Le Parisien pour préciser le sens de sa démarche. Dans cet échange, Teddy Riner revendique une volonté de rassemblement sans affiliation partisane, affirmant vouloir devenir un « président qui fédère » sans se situer à droite ou à gauche.

En effet, le champion tricolore a expliqué que sa décision avait mûri dans le sillage direct de l'élan collectif suscité par les Jeux olympiques de Paris 2024. Porté par cette dynamique populaire, il entend désormais transposer cette ferveur sur le plan politique. Ainsi, son mouvement politique refuse d'entrer dans les clivages traditionnels pour privilégier une adhésion commune à l'échelle du pays. Un projet ambitieux.

Toutefois, cette ambition politique ne pousse pas Teddy Riner vers la retraite sportive. Sur le plan des compétitions, sa dernière apparition individuelle remonte aux Jeux olympiques de Paris en août 2024, suivie d'une participation sous les couleurs du PSG Judo en Ligue des champions en décembre 2024. Malgré cette nouvelle démarche politique, le quintuple champion olympique et onze fois champion du monde de judo demeure bel et bien un athlète en activité.

Au-delà de l'élection présidentielle de 2027, Teddy Riner a ainsi fait part de son souhait de participer aux Jeux olympiques de Los Angeles 2028 si ses futures responsabilités et les Français le lui permettent. Le judoka français lie donc son avenir sportif à la suite de son engagement public, avec en ligne de mire les Jeux olympiques de Los Angeles 2028.

#2254. Mamadou Coulibaly (AS Monaco) s'excuse après son geste polémique

Auteur de son premier but dans l'élite lors du match nul sur le terrain sarthois, Mamadou Coulibaly a suscité une vive polémique en mimant un geste d'égorgement. Le jeune milieu de l'AS Monaco se retrouve désormais sous la menace directe d'une lourde suspension.

Dimanche 8 novembre 2026, l'AS Monaco a été accrochée sur la pelouse du Mans FC (1-1) pour le compte de la 10e journée de Ligue 1. Si le club du Rocher espérait engranger des points précieux, la rencontre a été marquée par le geste de son joueur de 22 ans.

Titularisé dans l'entrejeu monégasque, Mamadou Coulibaly pensait passer une bonne soirée après avoir ouvert le score à la 40e minute de jeu. Mais la joie d'inscrire son tout premier but en Ligue 1 a rapidement viré au malaise lors de sa célébration. Le milieu monégasque s'est en effet dirigé vers le poteau de corner pour glisser sur les genoux, avant de tirer la langue et de mimer un geste d'égorgement avec son pouce sous la gorge. Un geste polémique.

Ce geste n'est malheureusement pas inédit en Principauté cette saison. En effet, cette célébration reproduit à l'identique celle effectuée par son partenaire de club Paris Brunner face à Strasbourg en septembre dernier, lors de la 4e journée de Ligue 1. Pour ce geste, le jeune attaquant avait été sanctionné par la commission de discipline de la LFP de quatre matches de suspension, dont deux fermes et deux avec sursis.

D'ailleurs, cette récidive au sein du même effectif met le joueur dans une situation extrêmement délicate. En raison de ce précédent disciplinaire très récent, Mamadou Coulibaly risque une sanction similaire d'au moins deux matches de suspension ferme devant la commission de discipline de la LFP. L'instance disciplinaire devrait se saisir rapidement du dossier pour trancher sur ce comportement. Un coup dur en perspective.

Conscient des remous provoqués par son geste, le joueur de 22 ans s'est exprimé devant les journalistes à l'issue de la rencontre. « Je tiens à m’excuser parce que j’ai cru comprendre que ça avait été mal compris. C’est un geste que j’ai fait par rapport un ami à moi qui m’a conseillé au sujet de la célébration. Je tiens à m'excuser si ça a pu heurter des gens. Maintenant, ce sont des choses qui se font dans d'autres championnats, je n'ai pas pensé que ça pourrait avoir des conséquences négatives », a ainsi déclaré Mamadou Coulibaly en zone mixte au micro des médias après Le Mans-Monaco.

Toutefois, cette justification risque de ne pas suffire auprès de son entraîneur Filipe Luis. Le coach monégasque avait pourtant publiquement averti son groupe en septembre après l'affaire similaire, insistant sur l'exemplarité attendue : « Il a fait une erreur. Il l'a compris tout de suite. Il s'est excusé. Le club prend cette affaire très au sérieux (…). On espère que ça ne se reproduira plus », avait prévenu le technicien en conférence de presse. Au-delà de cette polémique extra-sportive, l'AS Monaco accueillera l'AJ Auxerre au stade Louis-II le samedi 21 novembre 2026 à 20h45 pour la 11e journée de Ligue 1.

#2252. Cold comfort: Dutch skater smashes one-hour natural ice record

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Marathon skater Judith Krabbenborg has taken advantage of the early cold snap to smash the world one-hour record for skating on natural ice.

The 29-year-old took to the ice in Winterswijk, Gelderland, at 8.30am on Tuesday and covered a distance of 34 kilometres 878 metres, beating the previous women’s record by 2.6 kilometres.

“I could feel my legs and my back hurting at the end, but there were so many people standing on the sidelines that it gave me energy,” she told NOS.

The previous record, set four years ago by Femke Mossinkoff, also in Winterswijk, was the first official time for the hour by a woman on natural ice. On the same day Bart Vreugdenhil set the current men’s record of 36 kilometers 717 metres.

Most skating records are set on artificial ice, but Winterswijk is one of three arenas where the local club has been able to create a natural arena for early-morning skaters during the recent cold weather.

The all-time women’s record is 40 kilometres 569 metres, set by another Dutch skater, Carien Kleibeuker, in Inzell, Austria, in 2015.

The rink at Winterswijk closed a few hours after Krabbenborg’s record-breaking skate as the ice started to melt, but it is expected to be available again on Wednesday morning.

#2251. Équipe de France : Zidane appelle un joueur de deuxième division !

C'est la surprise majeure de ce rassemblement de novembre. Pour pallier le forfait sur blessure de Pierre Kalulu, le sélectionneur Zinédine Zidane a décidé d'appeler pour la première fois Saël Kumbedi en équipe de France ce lundi 9 novembre 2026. Une trajectoire fulgurante pour le latéral droit évoluant en deuxième division allemande.

Le premier jour du rassemblement à Clairefontaine a réservé un rebondissement de taille dans les rangs tricolores. En effet, Pierre Kalulu a dû déclarer forfait en raison d'une lésion aux ischios du membre inférieur gauche contractée ce week-end avec la Juventus. Face à ce coup dur dans le couloir droit, Zinédine Zidane s'est tourné vers Saël Kumbedi, alors que le joueur de 21 ans avait initialement été retenu par Gérald Baticle avec les Espoirs pour disputer deux rencontres amicales face à l'Italie à Brest et contre l'Angleterre à Bristol.

Comptant déjà neuf sélections avec les Bleuets, le défenseur franchit donc un cap décisif. Pour régler la pénurie à ce poste, le staff technique a délibérément privilégié la jeunesse du joueur de Wolfsburg plutôt que l'expérience de Jonathan Clauss, pourtant performant à Nice du haut de ses 34 ans et de ses quatorze sélections (2 buts). Un choix fort.

Avant d'atteindre le groupe de Zinédine Zidane, Saël Kumbedi a connu un parcours jalonné d'étapes intenses. Formé au Havre, le natif de Stains avait rejoint l'Olympique Lyonnais en 2022 contre un chèque d'un million d'euros, découvrant la Ligue 1 à seulement 17 ans sous la houlette de Peter Bosz avant d'enchaîner sous les ordres de Laurent Blanc. Au total, le défenseur a disputé 62 matches sous le maillot des Gones, dont quatre apparitions sur la scène européenne en Ligue Europa lors de l'exercice 2024-2025.

Prêté au VfL Wolfsburg à l'été 2025, le latéral a ensuite été transféré définitivement le 1er janvier 2026 pour un montant de six millions d'euros. Toutefois, l'institution allemande, championne de Bundesliga en 2009 et installée dans l'élite sans discontinuer depuis 1997, a été reléguée en mai 2026 lors d'un barrage contre Paderborn (0-0, 2-1 a.p.). Un coup dur pour la formation allemande. Resté fidèle au club en 2. Bundesliga, Kumbedi est rapidement devenu un titulaire indiscutable sous les ordres de Tobias Strobl, malgré une blessure musculaire lui ayant fait manquer trois matches.

Cette convocation inattendue concrétise la passerelle directe établie entre la sélection A et les moins de 21 ans tricolores. Présent chez les Espoirs, le défenseur Nathan Zézé avait d'ailleurs rapporté le discours tenu par Zinédine Zidane :

Le parcours de Saël Kumbedi en équipe de France démarre ainsi dans ce contexte de renouvellement. Les Bleus disputeront deux matches de Ligue des nations capitaux au cours de cette trêve internationale. Ils commenceront par un déplacement face à l'Italie à Milan, programmé le jeudi 12 novembre 2026 à 20h45. L'équipe de France recevra ensuite la Turquie à Bordeaux, le dimanche 15 novembre 2026 à 20h45.
